========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Rules? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them rules, but I definitely have a routine. Most days I just stick to coffee in the morning. Keeps me moving. Then later on, I might grab a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever the guys ordered for the shop. It's just easier that way. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple routine, you know? 

Speaking of the guys ordering food, we've got this new kid, Tyler, started as a lube tech a couple months ago. Good kid, works hard, but his diet is unbelievable. I swear, I watch him eat a double bacon cheeseburger, large fries, and a liter of soda for lunch every single day. And he's skinny as a rail! It's crazy what your metabolism can do when you're nineteen. But the worst part is the smell. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. I've told him a hundred times to throw his trash in the dumpster out back, but it's like talking to a brick wall. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. 

Like the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. I yelled across the bay, dropped what I was doing, and practically tackled him before he cracked the thing. You crack one of those on a European car, you're waiting three days for the dealership to get the part in, and the customer is screaming at you the whole time. You gotta have a feel for the tools, you know? Hand-tighten the plastic stuff, always. But yeah, as far as my food goes, I just keep it simple. Coffee, maybe half a sandwich. It just works for me.

We’ve been so busy lately, especially with everyone needing their AC fixed before summer really hits. I spent three hours just trying to find a leak on this old Chevy yesterday. But yeah, the work doc made a big deal out of it, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing weight without trying, or without any changes to your diet or activity level?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's without trying, or that nothing's changed. I mean, I guess I've adjusted things a bit. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Which, by the way, I actually had to bring my own little coffee maker into the garage because Jimmy—he's our lube tech—he never cleans the communal pot. I swear it was starting to taste like straight 10W-30. \n\nSo I just have my own coffee, and then maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever is laying around later in the day. It's just... I don't know, with the shop running late all the time and the house being so busy, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules, you know? Keeps my head clear. Plus, my youngest is in travel baseball now, so the house is always a madhouse. We're constantly driving to tournaments all over the state on the weekends, which means I'm usually just driving and dodging all the overpriced concession stand food anyway. Have you seen what they charge for a plain hot dog at those parks now? It's absolute robbery. But yeah, I just keep my eating pretty simple and strict these days.\n\nDoctor: Has your eating pattern or the way you feel about your weight caused you any distress or affected your ability to function normally at work, home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Well, function normally? Oh, yeah, I'm still keeping up with the garage perfectly fine. If anything, I'm moving faster. We actually had this massive rush of brake jobs this week—must be all that rain we got over the weekend, people suddenly realizing they can't stop on wet pavement. Jimmy dropped a whole rotor on his boot on Tuesday, which was a complete mess and set us back a couple hours, but anyway... work is good. And I'm still keeping up with my basketball league, too. We actually won by twelve points on Sunday. \n\nBut, I mean... I guess it has caused some tension at home, if I'm being honest. It's mostly because I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals now. My wife always wants everyone at the dining room table at exactly 6 PM, and she makes these huge, heavy dinners. Like, she does this baked ziti, and she buys that giant block of mozzarella from the wholesale club—you know the one, it takes up half the fridge drawer and takes forever to grate. I just don't want to sit there and eat all that. So I skip it or just do my own thing, and now everyone keeps asking if I’m okay. My sister-in-law was over last week and wouldn't stop looking at me and bringing it up. It's just... I'm fine, you know? I just don't want the ziti.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts about your weight or shape influence how you see yourself overall, or affect how you feel about your worth as a person?\n\nPatient: Worth as a person? I mean, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't really sit around analyzing my shape or anything. I'm just a regular guy, you know? My worth is more about... well, just being a hard worker and providing for my family. Being good at what I do. \n\nLike, just this morning, we had this '68 Mustang roll into the shop. Beautiful car, cherry red, original interior. The owner was this older guy who couldn't get it to idle right to save his life. He actually took it to three different places before Jimmy told him to bring it to me. Those older carburetors, you gotta have a real feel for them, you know? You can't just plug a computer under the dashboard and let a screen tell you what's wrong like you do with these new cars. I swear, half the kids coming out of tech school these days don't even know what a distributor cap looks like. They just stare at their little diagnostic tablets and scratch their heads if there isn't a code. But yeah, I spent a good hour just tuning that carburetor by ear, getting the fuel-air mixture just perfect. It hums like a sewing machine now. The guy was thrilled. So, I mean, that's the kind of stuff that makes me feel good about myself. Not really... whatever you were saying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you’re overweight, even when others say you’re too thin or when you can see or feel that you’ve lost weight?\n\nPatient: Overweight? No, not really. I mean, I know I've lost some weight, but I don't look in the mirror and think I'm fat or anything. People keep saying I'm too thin, especially my wife, but she says that about everyone. She actually tried to tell my buddy Dave he was looking too thin the other day. Dave! The guy easily pushes two-fifty. \n\nDave and I actually go way back, we used to work at this old Chevy dealership out on Route 9 before it got bought out by one of those giant mega-dealer groups. You know the ones, where they have like five different brands on one lot and the service writers wear these stiff little ties and try to upsell you on a cabin air filter for eighty bucks. It's ridiculous. Dave ended up leaving there right after the buyout because they wanted him to start hitting these crazy quotas for upselling wiper blades and fluid flushes. He’s a good mechanic, he just wants to fix cars, not be a salesman. He ended up opening his own small shop out by the county line. Anyway, yeah, I don't feel like I'm overweight at all. I just like keeping things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise excessively to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Throw up? God, no. I hate throwing up. I haven't done that since... well, actually, it was a couple years ago. Jimmy and I got these chili dogs from that food truck that parks down by the wholesale parts warehouse on 4th street. You know the one? It's got this faded picture of a hot dog wearing sunglasses on the side of it. Anyway, I told Jimmy the meat looked a little gray, but he was starving because we'd been pulling a transmission all morning on this old F-150. Man, that truck was an absolute nightmare. Rusted bolts everywhere, took half a can of penetrating oil just to get the mount loose. But yeah, we ate those dogs and both of us were out sick the entire next day. Worst twenty-four hours of my life. So no, I definitely don't make myself do that on purpose. \n\nAnd laxatives? No way. Never touched them. And as for exercise, I just play in my regular basketball league, like I was saying before. I'm not doing anything crazy to lose weight. Though, speaking of the league, we're actually playing the guys from the county fire department next week, and they always play dirty. Last season, their point guard threw an elbow that nearly broke my nose, and the ref didn't even call it. It's like they get a free pass just because they drive the big red trucks. But yeah, no, I just play my normal games. I'm not running marathons or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating certain foods or restrict your eating beyond what you've already described because you're afraid of gaining weight or because of how it might affect your shape?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I wouldn't say I avoid foods just to avoid them, but... yeah, if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling even a little tighter around the waist, I do get kind of... I don't know, I just start thinking I'm getting soft. Like, if I'm carrying extra weight, I'm going to be slower moving around the shop, sliding under cars, or even just getting up and down the court during league games. \n\nActually, speaking of jeans, I had to take back this pair of Levi's I bought last weekend. They changed the sizing or something, because I always buy the exact same style—the 501s, you know, the classic ones—and they just fit completely wrong now. It's like they're trying to save money on denim by cutting them tighter in the legs. I was talking to the guy at the hardware store about it, and he said the same thing happened with his work boots. He bought the same brand for fifteen years, and suddenly the soles started wearing out in six months because they moved production overseas. It's just like what's happening with car parts, honestly. You buy a replacement water pump now, and half the time the casting is so cheap it cracks when you torque the bolts down. You really have to hunt down the old stock if you want something that's going to last. But anyway, yeah, I just don't want to get soft and slow down, so I keep an eye on things.\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep an eye on things to avoid getting soft, how much of your focus is on your weight or shape compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Compared to how I used to be? I don't really know... I haven't really measured it or anything like that. I guess I just pay attention to it the same as I always do. I haven't noticed a big difference in how much I think about it. \n\nBut man, speaking of how things used to be, working on cars used to be so much simpler. Back when I started out, you could actually open a hood and see the engine, you know? Now? It's all just giant plastic engine covers and a million computer sensors. We had this 2019 Audi in the shop yesterday, and just to get to the oil filter, you have to take off this massive undercarriage shield. It's held on by about fifteen different Torx screws, and half of them were completely stripped out because the last guy who did the lube job used an impact driver on them. I mean, who uses an impact gun on a plastic skid plate? It's just pure laziness. \n\nThe younger guys coming into the shop now, they don't want to take the time to do things by hand or actually feel the threads catch. They just want to plug in the scanner and let the iPad tell them what part to swap out. There's no real diagnosing anymore, it's just throwing parts at a code. It drives me crazy. But anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, I don't know, I just keep an eye on it. I wouldn't say it's some big new focus or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any medical problems or been diagnosed with a condition that could explain your weight loss, such as thyroid issues, diabetes, or gastrointestinal problems?\n\nPatient: Medical problems? No, nothing like that. I've never had any thyroid stuff or diabetes or stomach issues. I'm pretty healthy, all things considered. I mean, my back gives me some trouble now and then, but that's just from leaning over engine bays for twenty years. \n\nActually, speaking of medical stuff, did you hear about that new clinic they opened up over on Elm Street? The one in the old strip mall where the Blockbuster used to be? I drove past it the other day, and they've got this huge glowing sign now. It's so weird seeing it like that, because I remember renting VHS tapes from that exact spot when I was a kid. You'd go in on a Friday night, and if you didn't get there by six, all the new releases were gone. Just empty plastic cases sitting on the shelf mocking you. My buddy Dave used to work there, and he'd always stash a copy of whatever the big action movie was behind the counter for us. Dave actually moved out to Arizona a few years back. Bought a house with a pool and everything. He says the dry heat is great, but I don't know how anyone deals with the sun baking the clear coat right off your car out there. You see these cars from out West, and the paint is just peeling off in sheets on the hood and roof. It's a real shame, especially on the classics. But yeah, no, I don't have any medical conditions or anything causing the weight loss. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself sick or done anything else to get rid of food after eating because you were worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Make myself sick? Like, throw up? No, no, I've never done anything like that. That just seems... I don't know, it's not something I'd do. I mean, the last time I threw up was probably five years ago when I got food poisoning from this taco truck that parked outside the shop. \n\nMan, that was brutal. It was this new truck, bright yellow, and they had these al pastor tacos that smelled amazing. The guys and I decided to try it out for lunch. Big mistake. Half the shop was out the next day. It's funny, though, because the food truck business has just exploded around here lately. Used to be you'd only see them at construction sites, you know, the old \"roach coaches.\" Now they're everywhere, charging like fifteen bucks for a gourmet grilled cheese. My wife loves going to those food truck festivals they have downtown on the weekends. They block off the whole street, and it's just packed with people. Finding parking is a nightmare, though. Last time we went, I had to park like six blocks away in this sketchy alley, and I spent the whole time worrying someone was going to sideswipe my truck. It's a Chevy Silverado, extended cab, so it sticks out a bit. People just don't know how to navigate tight spaces anymore. They rely entirely on those backup cameras, and half the time they're not even looking in their mirrors. I see it all the time at the shop—bumpers all scuffed up because someone backed into a pole while staring at their dashboard screen. But anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, making myself sick. No, never done that.\n\nDoctor: Even though you say you haven't felt overweight, do you ever feel like your body looks different or that certain parts of you are bigger than they should be?\n\nPatient: Bigger than they should be? No, not really. I mean, I look in the mirror and I just look like me, you know? I haven't noticed any parts looking weird or out of proportion. I just try to keep everything tight so I don't get soft. \n\nBut you know what is getting bigger than it should be? The size of these new pickup trucks. It's ridiculous. Have you seen the new heavy-duty models they're putting out? The grilles on those things are practically at eye level for a normal person. We had a guy bring in a 2023 Ram 2500 last week for a brake job, and I swear, I almost needed a stepladder just to check under the hood. And the blind spots on those things! It's no wonder people are always backing into things. The beds are so high up now, you can't even reach over the side to grab a toolbox without pulling a muscle. \n\nBack in the day, a work truck was just that—a work truck. You had your standard cab, an eight-foot bed, and it sat low enough that you could actually load sheets of plywood into it without needing a forklift. Now they're all lifted from the factory, with these massive crew cabs that are nicer inside than my living room. Heated leather seats, giant touchscreens, panoramic sunroofs... it's like driving a luxury yacht down the highway. And the price tags! Guys are taking out eighty-month loans just to afford a truck they're going to use to commute to an office job. It makes no sense. If you're going to haul air, buy a sedan, right? But what was the question? Oh, my body looking different. No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Just staying regular.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in your appetite, energy level, or digestion recently, like feeling full quickly, nausea, or changes in your bowel habits?\n\nPatient: Changes in appetite or energy? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My digestion is fine, no nausea or anything. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, you wouldn't believe the energy it takes to deal with some of the parts suppliers these days. It used to be you'd call up the local auto parts store, talk to a guy named Bob who'd been there for thirty years, and he'd have the exact alternator you needed sitting on the counter by the time you drove over. Now? Everything's centralized in these massive distribution warehouses. You order a part online, and it's a total crapshoot whether the right thing actually shows up. \n\nJust last Tuesday, I ordered a set of rotors for a Honda Civic. Simple, right? Most common car on the road. The delivery guy shows up, drops off the boxes, and when I open them up, it's a pair of brake drums for a 1998 Ford Ranger. I mean, how do you even mix that up? They don't even look the same! So then you have to get on the phone, wait on hold for twenty minutes listening to that terrible elevator music, just to talk to some customer service rep who doesn't know a spark plug from a lug nut. It just drains the life out of you, dealing with the incompetence. You try to run an efficient shop, keep the bays turning over, but you're constantly bottlenecked by these massive corporate supply chains that can't get a simple order right. It's exhausting. But yeah, physically, my energy is fine. No issues with my stomach or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever weighed yourself recently, and if so, do you know if your weight is below what would be considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, that's kind of why I'm here. I had to do this work physical recently, and the doc there told me my weight was too low for my height. But I don't really see it as a bad thing. I feel a lot lighter on my feet, you know? Makes a huge difference when I'm playing pickup basketball on the weekends, and I definitely don't feel as weighed down when I'm moving around the garage all day. \n\nSpeaking of the garage, man, the floors in that place are brutal on your knees after a while. Concrete just doesn't give. A buddy of mine, Dave, he's been turning wrenches for thirty years, and his knees are completely shot. He just had to get one replaced last month. The recovery on that is no joke. They had him up and walking on it the same day, which sounds crazy to me, but I guess that's how they do it now. He was telling me about the physical therapy, how they basically force you to bend it even when it feels like it's going to snap. Sounds like torture. He's got this machine he has to use at home that just slowly bends and straightens his leg for hours. His wife is going nuts because it makes this constant whining noise. She's been spending half her time out in her garden just to get away from the sound. She grows these massive heirloom tomatoes, though. Brought a basket into the shop before Dave's surgery, and they were incredible. Nothing like a real, homegrown tomato. The ones at the grocery store just taste like water. But anyway, yeah, the physical doc said I was under whatever the normal weight is supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever follow any specific dietary rules or routines to make sure you don’t gain weight, like weighing or measuring your food, avoiding certain ingredients, or tracking calories?\n\nPatient: Rules? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them rules, but I definitely have a routine. Most days I just stick to coffee in the morning. Keeps me moving. Then later on, I might grab a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever the guys ordered for the shop. It's just easier that way. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple routine, you know? \n\nSpeaking of the guys ordering food, we've got this new kid, Tyler, started as a lube tech a couple months ago. Good kid, works hard, but his diet is unbelievable. I swear, I watch him eat a double bacon cheeseburger, large fries, and a liter of soda for lunch every single day. And he's skinny as a rail! It's crazy what your metabolism can do when you're nineteen. But the worst part is the smell. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. I've told him a hundred times to throw his trash in the dumpster out back, but it's like talking to a brick wall. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. \n\nLike the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. I yelled across the bay, dropped what I was doing, and practically tackled him before he cracked the thing. You crack one of those on a European car, you're waiting three days for the dealership to get the part in, and the customer is screaming at you the whole time. You gotta have a feel for the tools, you know? Hand-tighten the plastic stuff, always. But yeah, as far as my food goes, I just keep it simple. Coffee, maybe half a sandwich. It just works for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It used to be you'd call up the local auto parts store, talk to a guy named Bob who'd been there for thirty years, and he'd have the exact alternator you needed sitting on the counter by the time you drove over. Now? Everything's centralized in these massive distribution warehouses. He's got this machine he has to use at home that just slowly bends and straightens his leg for hours. His wife is going nuts because it makes this constant whining noise. She's been spending half her time out in her garden just to get away from the sound. She grows these massive heirloom tomatoes, though. Brought a basket into the shop before Dave's surgery, and they were incredible. Nothing like a real, homegrown tomato. The ones at the grocery store just taste like water. Then later on, I might grab a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever the guys ordered for the shop. It's just easier that way. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple routine, you know? \n\nSpeaking of the guys ordering food, we've got this new kid, Tyler, started as a lube tech a couple months ago. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. \n\nLike the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean, I guess I'm trying to keep it down, yeah. It's not like I'm trying to vanish or anything, but if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tight around the waist, I start getting this thought in my head that I'm getting soft. Like, I'll be slower getting up and down the court, or I won't be able to maneuver around the cars as easy. You really gotta be able to contort yourself when you're working under a dash, you know? 

Man, working under the dash is the absolute worst. They cram so much wiring and so many modules up there now, you practically need to be a gymnast just to change a heater core. I had this Chevy Silverado in last week, right? Customer says the blend door actuator is clicking. Classic issue. But to get to the thing, you have to pull the entire dash assembly apart. And I'm laying on my back across the driver's seat, my head down by the pedals, trying to reach up blind with a quarter-inch ratchet to get this one tiny screw out. And of course, the screw strips. They use these cheap, soft metal screws that strip if you even look at them wrong. So now I'm stuck, upside down, blood rushing to my head, trying to figure out how to drill out a screw in a space where I can't even fit my hand. 

Took me three hours just to get that one part out. My neck was stiff for two days after that. I ended up having to use my wife's heating pad just to be able to turn my head enough to check my blind spot driving to work. She wasn't thrilled about that, she uses it for her lower back. She's been doing this new yoga class at the community center, and I think she pulled something trying to do one of those crazy poses. She says it's supposed to be relaxing, but she always comes home complaining about how much she hurts. I don't get it. But yeah, if I put on weight, I'd just feel too sluggish to deal with all that awkward positioning at the shop.

No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My digestion is fine, no nausea or anything. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, you wouldn't believe the energy it takes to deal with some of the parts suppliers these days. It used to be you'd call up the local auto parts store, talk to a guy named Bob who'd been there for thirty years, and he'd have the exact alternator you needed sitting on the counter by the time you drove over. Now? Everything's centralized in these massive distribution warehouses. You order a part online, and it's a total crapshoot whether the right thing actually shows up. \n\nJust last Tuesday, I ordered a set of rotors for a Honda Civic. Simple, right? Most common car on the road. The delivery guy shows up, drops off the boxes, and when I open them up, it's a pair of brake drums for a 1998 Ford Ranger. I mean, how do you even mix that up? They don't even look the same! So then you have to get on the phone, wait on hold for twenty minutes listening to that terrible elevator music, just to talk to some customer service rep who doesn't know a spark plug from a lug nut. It just drains the life out of you, dealing with the incompetence. You try to run an efficient shop, keep the bays turning over, but you're constantly bottlenecked by these massive corporate supply chains that can't get a simple order right. It's exhausting. But yeah, physically, my energy is fine. No issues with my stomach or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever weighed yourself recently, and if so, do you know if your weight is below what would be considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, that's kind of why I'm here. I had to do this work physical recently, and the doc there told me my weight was too low for my height. But I don't really see it as a bad thing. I feel a lot lighter on my feet, you know? Makes a huge difference when I'm playing pickup basketball on the weekends, and I definitely don't feel as weighed down when I'm moving around the garage all day. \n\nSpeaking of the garage, man, the floors in that place are brutal on your knees after a while. Concrete just doesn't give. A buddy of mine, Dave, he's been turning wrenches for thirty years, and his knees are completely shot. He just had to get one replaced last month. The recovery on that is no joke. They had him up and walking on it the same day, which sounds crazy to me, but I guess that's how they do it now. He was telling me about the physical therapy, how they basically force you to bend it even when it feels like it's going to snap. Sounds like torture. He's got this machine he has to use at home that just slowly bends and straightens his leg for hours. His wife is going nuts because it makes this constant whining noise. She's been spending half her time out in her garden just to get away from the sound. She grows these massive heirloom tomatoes, though. Brought a basket into the shop before Dave's surgery, and they were incredible. Nothing like a real, homegrown tomato. The ones at the grocery store just taste like water. But anyway, yeah, the physical doc said I was under whatever the normal weight is supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever follow any specific dietary rules or routines to make sure you don’t gain weight, like weighing or measuring your food, avoiding certain ingredients, or tracking calories?\n\nPatient: Rules? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them rules, but I definitely have a routine. Most days I just stick to coffee in the morning. Keeps me moving. Then later on, I might grab a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever the guys ordered for the shop. It's just easier that way. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple routine, you know? \n\nSpeaking of the guys ordering food, we've got this new kid, Tyler, started as a lube tech a couple months ago. Good kid, works hard, but his diet is unbelievable. I swear, I watch him eat a double bacon cheeseburger, large fries, and a liter of soda for lunch every single day. And he's skinny as a rail! It's crazy what your metabolism can do when you're nineteen. But the worst part is the smell. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. I've told him a hundred times to throw his trash in the dumpster out back, but it's like talking to a brick wall. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. \n\nLike the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. I yelled across the bay, dropped what I was doing, and practically tackled him before he cracked the thing. You crack one of those on a European car, you're waiting three days for the dealership to get the part in, and the customer is screaming at you the whole time. You gotta have a feel for the tools, you know? Hand-tighten the plastic stuff, always. But yeah, as far as my food goes, I just keep it simple. Coffee, maybe half a sandwich. It just works for me.\n\nDoctor: Even though you feel good and are keeping up with work and basketball, is your weight loss something you're actively trying to achieve, or has it happened more than you intended?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I guess I'm trying to keep it down, yeah. It's not like I'm trying to vanish or anything, but if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tight around the waist, I start getting this thought in my head that I'm getting soft. Like, I'll be slower getting up and down the court, or I won't be able to maneuver around the cars as easy. You really gotta be able to contort yourself when you're working under a dash, you know? \n\nMan, working under the dash is the absolute worst. Customer says the blend door actuator is clicking. Classic issue. But to get to the thing, you have to pull the entire dash assembly apart. And I'm laying on my back across the driver's seat, my head down by the pedals, trying to reach up blind with a quarter-inch ratchet to get this one tiny screw out. And of course, the screw strips. They use these cheap, soft metal screws that strip if you even look at them wrong. She says it's supposed to be relaxing, but she always comes home complaining about how much she hurts. I don't get it. But yeah, if I put on weight, I'd just feel too sluggish to deal with all that awkward positioning at the shop.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight loss was completely intentional, or did you lose more weight than you originally aimed for?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I had a specific number in mind, honestly. I just knew I wanted to lean out a bit, and I kept going until I felt right. I guess I haven't really thought about whether I lost more than I planned. I just kind of keep an eye on how my clothes fit and how I feel on the court. \n\nSpeaking of the court, we had this insane game last Sunday. We play at this outdoor court down by the middle school, and the asphalt is getting pretty chewed up in places. But they had this one guy, tall, maybe six-four, who kept trying to post me up. I might not be the biggest guy out there, especially now, but I know how to hold my ground. \n\nHe goes up for a rebound, right? And he comes down awkward on one of those patched spots on the asphalt. You could hear the pop from half-court. Rolled his ankle so bad he couldn't even put weight on it. We had to help him hobble over to his car. It's crazy how fast an injury like that can happen. Makes you appreciate being light on your feet, you know? Less weight coming down on those joints. That's what I keep telling my buddy Dave with his new knee. He needs to drop a few pounds if he wants that hardware to last. He's a big guy, always has been. Loves his barbecue. Brings the leftovers into the shop on Mondays. Smells incredible, but man, that stuff is just pure fat and salt. I tell him he's gonna clog his arteries before that new knee even gets broken in, but he just laughs it off. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't really count calories or anything like that. I just have my rules, I guess. Like I said before, most days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever the guys ordered. It's just easier. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules, you know? It keeps things manageable. 

Speaking of the house being busy, my youngest, Leo, he just started playing the drums. And I mean, I love the kid, but he has zero rhythm right now. Just zero. He just bangs on the snare like he's trying to punish it. We had to move the drum kit out to the garage because the dog was having panic attacks every time he sat down to play. So now, I get home from a ten-hour shift listening to air compressors and impact guns all day, and I pull into the driveway, and it sounds like a construction site in my own garage. I tried to show him some basic beats, you know, just a simple four-four time, but he just wants to hit the cymbals as hard as he can. I'm thinking about getting him some of those practice pads that muffle the sound, or maybe just soundproofing the garage doors. I saw this guy on YouTube who used old moving blankets and some acoustic foam to soundproof his garage for a band practice space. Seemed pretty straightforward. You just need a heavy-duty staple gun and some patience. I might have to run to the hardware store this weekend and see what they have. But yeah, sticking to my food rules just helps me deal with all the chaos, I guess.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.01, R63.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, it's actually my work physical that got me here. The doctor there was going on about how my weight is too low. Which, honestly, I don't really get. I mean, I feel great. I just feel lighter on my feet, you know? Like when I play basketball on the weekends with the guys from the shop. We play over at the community center. It’s a good group, mostly guys I’ve known since high school. Anyway, being lighter just makes it easier to move around the court. And at the garage, too—I’m not as weighed down when I’m getting under the cars or reaching for tools. We’ve been so busy lately, especially with everyone needing their AC fixed before summer really hits. I spent three hours just trying to find a leak on this old Chevy yesterday. But yeah, the work doc made a big deal out of it, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing weight without trying, or without any changes to your diet or activity level?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's without trying, or that nothing's changed. I mean, I guess I've adjusted things a bit. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Which, by the way, I actually had to bring my own little coffee maker into the garage because Jimmy—he's our lube tech—he never cleans the communal pot. I swear it was starting to taste like straight 10W-30. \n\nSo I just have my own coffee, and then maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever is laying around later in the day. It's just... I don't know, with the shop running late all the time and the house being so busy, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules, you know? Keeps my head clear. Plus, my youngest is in travel baseball now, so the house is always a madhouse. We're constantly driving to tournaments all over the state on the weekends, which means I'm usually just driving and dodging all the overpriced concession stand food anyway. Have you seen what they charge for a plain hot dog at those parks now? It's absolute robbery. But yeah, I just keep my eating pretty simple and strict these days.\n\nDoctor: Has your eating pattern or the way you feel about your weight caused you any distress or affected your ability to function normally at work, home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Well, function normally? Oh, yeah, I'm still keeping up with the garage perfectly fine. If anything, I'm moving faster. We actually had this massive rush of brake jobs this week—must be all that rain we got over the weekend, people suddenly realizing they can't stop on wet pavement. Jimmy dropped a whole rotor on his boot on Tuesday, which was a complete mess and set us back a couple hours, but anyway... work is good. And I'm still keeping up with my basketball league, too. We actually won by twelve points on Sunday. \n\nBut, I mean... I guess it has caused some tension at home, if I'm being honest. It's mostly because I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals now. My wife always wants everyone at the dining room table at exactly 6 PM, and she makes these huge, heavy dinners. Like, she does this baked ziti, and she buys that giant block of mozzarella from the wholesale club—you know the one, it takes up half the fridge drawer and takes forever to grate. I just don't want to sit there and eat all that. So I skip it or just do my own thing, and now everyone keeps asking if I’m okay. My sister-in-law was over last week and wouldn't stop looking at me and bringing it up. It's just... I'm fine, you know? I just don't want the ziti.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts about your weight or shape influence how you see yourself overall, or affect how you feel about your worth as a person?\n\nPatient: Worth as a person? I mean, no, I wouldn't say that. I don't really sit around analyzing my shape or anything. I'm just a regular guy, you know? My worth is more about... well, just being a hard worker and providing for my family. Being good at what I do. \n\nLike, just this morning, we had this '68 Mustang roll into the shop. Beautiful car, cherry red, original interior. The owner was this older guy who couldn't get it to idle right to save his life. He actually took it to three different places before Jimmy told him to bring it to me. Those older carburetors, you gotta have a real feel for them, you know? You can't just plug a computer under the dashboard and let a screen tell you what's wrong like you do with these new cars. I swear, half the kids coming out of tech school these days don't even know what a distributor cap looks like. They just stare at their little diagnostic tablets and scratch their heads if there isn't a code. But yeah, I spent a good hour just tuning that carburetor by ear, getting the fuel-air mixture just perfect. It hums like a sewing machine now. The guy was thrilled. So, I mean, that's the kind of stuff that makes me feel good about myself. Not really... whatever you were saying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you’re overweight, even when others say you’re too thin or when you can see or feel that you’ve lost weight?\n\nPatient: Overweight? No, not really. I mean, I know I've lost some weight, but I don't look in the mirror and think I'm fat or anything. People keep saying I'm too thin, especially my wife, but she says that about everyone. She actually tried to tell my buddy Dave he was looking too thin the other day. Dave! The guy easily pushes two-fifty. \n\nDave and I actually go way back, we used to work at this old Chevy dealership out on Route 9 before it got bought out by one of those giant mega-dealer groups. You know the ones, where they have like five different brands on one lot and the service writers wear these stiff little ties and try to upsell you on a cabin air filter for eighty bucks. It's ridiculous. Dave ended up leaving there right after the buyout because they wanted him to start hitting these crazy quotas for upselling wiper blades and fluid flushes. He’s a good mechanic, he just wants to fix cars, not be a salesman. He ended up opening his own small shop out by the county line. Anyway, yeah, I don't feel like I'm overweight at all. I just like keeping things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise excessively to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Throw up? God, no. I hate throwing up. I haven't done that since... well, actually, it was a couple years ago. Jimmy and I got these chili dogs from that food truck that parks down by the wholesale parts warehouse on 4th street. You know the one? It's got this faded picture of a hot dog wearing sunglasses on the side of it. Anyway, I told Jimmy the meat looked a little gray, but he was starving because we'd been pulling a transmission all morning on this old F-150. Man, that truck was an absolute nightmare. Rusted bolts everywhere, took half a can of penetrating oil just to get the mount loose. But yeah, we ate those dogs and both of us were out sick the entire next day. Worst twenty-four hours of my life. So no, I definitely don't make myself do that on purpose. \n\nAnd laxatives? No way. Never touched them. And as for exercise, I just play in my regular basketball league, like I was saying before. I'm not doing anything crazy to lose weight. Though, speaking of the league, we're actually playing the guys from the county fire department next week, and they always play dirty. Last season, their point guard threw an elbow that nearly broke my nose, and the ref didn't even call it. It's like they get a free pass just because they drive the big red trucks. But yeah, no, I just play my normal games. I'm not running marathons or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating certain foods or restrict your eating beyond what you've already described because you're afraid of gaining weight or because of how it might affect your shape?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I wouldn't say I avoid foods just to avoid them, but... yeah, if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling even a little tighter around the waist, I do get kind of... I don't know, I just start thinking I'm getting soft. Like, if I'm carrying extra weight, I'm going to be slower moving around the shop, sliding under cars, or even just getting up and down the court during league games. \n\nActually, speaking of jeans, I had to take back this pair of Levi's I bought last weekend. They changed the sizing or something, because I always buy the exact same style—the 501s, you know, the classic ones—and they just fit completely wrong now. It's like they're trying to save money on denim by cutting them tighter in the legs. I was talking to the guy at the hardware store about it, and he said the same thing happened with his work boots. He bought the same brand for fifteen years, and suddenly the soles started wearing out in six months because they moved production overseas. It's just like what's happening with car parts, honestly. You buy a replacement water pump now, and half the time the casting is so cheap it cracks when you torque the bolts down. You really have to hunt down the old stock if you want something that's going to last. But anyway, yeah, I just don't want to get soft and slow down, so I keep an eye on things.\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep an eye on things to avoid getting soft, how much of your focus is on your weight or shape compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Compared to how I used to be? I don't really know... I haven't really measured it or anything like that. I guess I just pay attention to it the same as I always do. I haven't noticed a big difference in how much I think about it. \n\nBut man, speaking of how things used to be, working on cars used to be so much simpler. Back when I started out, you could actually open a hood and see the engine, you know? Now? It's all just giant plastic engine covers and a million computer sensors. We had this 2019 Audi in the shop yesterday, and just to get to the oil filter, you have to take off this massive undercarriage shield. It's held on by about fifteen different Torx screws, and half of them were completely stripped out because the last guy who did the lube job used an impact driver on them. I mean, who uses an impact gun on a plastic skid plate? It's just pure laziness. \n\nThe younger guys coming into the shop now, they don't want to take the time to do things by hand or actually feel the threads catch. They just want to plug in the scanner and let the iPad tell them what part to swap out. There's no real diagnosing anymore, it's just throwing parts at a code. It drives me crazy. But anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, I don't know, I just keep an eye on it. I wouldn't say it's some big new focus or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any medical problems or been diagnosed with a condition that could explain your weight loss, such as thyroid issues, diabetes, or gastrointestinal problems?\n\nPatient: Medical problems? No, nothing like that. I've never had any thyroid stuff or diabetes or stomach issues. I'm pretty healthy, all things considered. I mean, my back gives me some trouble now and then, but that's just from leaning over engine bays for twenty years. \n\nActually, speaking of medical stuff, did you hear about that new clinic they opened up over on Elm Street? The one in the old strip mall where the Blockbuster used to be? I drove past it the other day, and they've got this huge glowing sign now. It's so weird seeing it like that, because I remember renting VHS tapes from that exact spot when I was a kid. You'd go in on a Friday night, and if you didn't get there by six, all the new releases were gone. Just empty plastic cases sitting on the shelf mocking you. My buddy Dave used to work there, and he'd always stash a copy of whatever the big action movie was behind the counter for us. Dave actually moved out to Arizona a few years back. Bought a house with a pool and everything. He says the dry heat is great, but I don't know how anyone deals with the sun baking the clear coat right off your car out there. You see these cars from out West, and the paint is just peeling off in sheets on the hood and roof. It's a real shame, especially on the classics. But yeah, no, I don't have any medical conditions or anything causing the weight loss. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself sick or done anything else to get rid of food after eating because you were worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Make myself sick? Like, throw up? No, no, I've never done anything like that. That just seems... I don't know, it's not something I'd do. I mean, the last time I threw up was probably five years ago when I got food poisoning from this taco truck that parked outside the shop. \n\nMan, that was brutal. It was this new truck, bright yellow, and they had these al pastor tacos that smelled amazing. The guys and I decided to try it out for lunch. Big mistake. Half the shop was out the next day. It's funny, though, because the food truck business has just exploded around here lately. Used to be you'd only see them at construction sites, you know, the old \"roach coaches.\" Now they're everywhere, charging like fifteen bucks for a gourmet grilled cheese. My wife loves going to those food truck festivals they have downtown on the weekends. They block off the whole street, and it's just packed with people. Finding parking is a nightmare, though. Last time we went, I had to park like six blocks away in this sketchy alley, and I spent the whole time worrying someone was going to sideswipe my truck. It's a Chevy Silverado, extended cab, so it sticks out a bit. People just don't know how to navigate tight spaces anymore. They rely entirely on those backup cameras, and half the time they're not even looking in their mirrors. I see it all the time at the shop—bumpers all scuffed up because someone backed into a pole while staring at their dashboard screen. But anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, making myself sick. No, never done that.\n\nDoctor: Even though you say you haven't felt overweight, do you ever feel like your body looks different or that certain parts of you are bigger than they should be?\n\nPatient: Bigger than they should be? No, not really. I mean, I look in the mirror and I just look like me, you know? I haven't noticed any parts looking weird or out of proportion. I just try to keep everything tight so I don't get soft. \n\nBut you know what is getting bigger than it should be? The size of these new pickup trucks. It's ridiculous. Have you seen the new heavy-duty models they're putting out? The grilles on those things are practically at eye level for a normal person. We had a guy bring in a 2023 Ram 2500 last week for a brake job, and I swear, I almost needed a stepladder just to check under the hood. And the blind spots on those things! It's no wonder people are always backing into things. The beds are so high up now, you can't even reach over the side to grab a toolbox without pulling a muscle. \n\nBack in the day, a work truck was just that—a work truck. You had your standard cab, an eight-foot bed, and it sat low enough that you could actually load sheets of plywood into it without needing a forklift. Now they're all lifted from the factory, with these massive crew cabs that are nicer inside than my living room. Heated leather seats, giant touchscreens, panoramic sunroofs... it's like driving a luxury yacht down the highway. And the price tags! Guys are taking out eighty-month loans just to afford a truck they're going to use to commute to an office job. It makes no sense. If you're going to haul air, buy a sedan, right? But what was the question? Oh, my body looking different. No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Just staying regular.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in your appetite, energy level, or digestion recently, like feeling full quickly, nausea, or changes in your bowel habits?\n\nPatient: Changes in appetite or energy? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My digestion is fine, no nausea or anything. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, you wouldn't believe the energy it takes to deal with some of the parts suppliers these days. It used to be you'd call up the local auto parts store, talk to a guy named Bob who'd been there for thirty years, and he'd have the exact alternator you needed sitting on the counter by the time you drove over. Now? Everything's centralized in these massive distribution warehouses. You order a part online, and it's a total crapshoot whether the right thing actually shows up. \n\nJust last Tuesday, I ordered a set of rotors for a Honda Civic. Simple, right? Most common car on the road. The delivery guy shows up, drops off the boxes, and when I open them up, it's a pair of brake drums for a 1998 Ford Ranger. I mean, how do you even mix that up? They don't even look the same! So then you have to get on the phone, wait on hold for twenty minutes listening to that terrible elevator music, just to talk to some customer service rep who doesn't know a spark plug from a lug nut. It just drains the life out of you, dealing with the incompetence. You try to run an efficient shop, keep the bays turning over, but you're constantly bottlenecked by these massive corporate supply chains that can't get a simple order right. It's exhausting. But yeah, physically, my energy is fine. No issues with my stomach or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever weighed yourself recently, and if so, do you know if your weight is below what would be considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, that's kind of why I'm here. I had to do this work physical recently, and the doc there told me my weight was too low for my height. But I don't really see it as a bad thing. I feel a lot lighter on my feet, you know? Makes a huge difference when I'm playing pickup basketball on the weekends, and I definitely don't feel as weighed down when I'm moving around the garage all day. \n\nSpeaking of the garage, man, the floors in that place are brutal on your knees after a while. Concrete just doesn't give. A buddy of mine, Dave, he's been turning wrenches for thirty years, and his knees are completely shot. He just had to get one replaced last month. The recovery on that is no joke. They had him up and walking on it the same day, which sounds crazy to me, but I guess that's how they do it now. He was telling me about the physical therapy, how they basically force you to bend it even when it feels like it's going to snap. Sounds like torture. He's got this machine he has to use at home that just slowly bends and straightens his leg for hours. His wife is going nuts because it makes this constant whining noise. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. I've told him a hundred times to throw his trash in the dumpster out back, but it's like talking to a brick wall. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. \n\nLike the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. I yelled across the bay, dropped what I was doing, and practically tackled him before he cracked the thing. You crack one of those on a European car, you're waiting three days for the dealership to get the part in, and the customer is screaming at you the whole time. You gotta have a feel for the tools, you know? Hand-tighten the plastic stuff, always. But yeah, as far as my food goes, I just keep it simple. Coffee, maybe half a sandwich. It just works for me.\n\nDoctor: Even though you feel good and are keeping up with work and basketball, is your weight loss something you're actively trying to achieve, or has it happened more than you intended?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I guess I'm trying to keep it down, yeah. Like, I'll be slower getting up and down the court, or I won't be able to maneuver around the cars as easy. Customer says the blend door actuator is clicking. Classic issue. But to get to the thing, you have to pull the entire dash assembly apart. And I'm laying on my back across the driver's seat, my head down by the pedals, trying to reach up blind with a quarter-inch ratchet to get this one tiny screw out. And of course, the screw strips. They use these cheap, soft metal screws that strip if you even look at them wrong. The focus on routine and simplicity in eating, without calorie counting or overt distress, supports an atypical eating disorder presentation rather than a more severe or classical eating disorder. Rusted bolts everywhere, took half a can of penetrating oil just to get the mount loose. But yeah, we ate those dogs and both of us were out sick the entire next day. Worst twenty-four hours of my life. So no, I definitely don't make myself do that on purpose. \n\nAnd laxatives? No way. Never touched them. And as for exercise, I just play in my regular basketball league, like I was saying before. I'm not doing anything crazy to lose weight. Though, speaking of the league, we're actually playing the guys from the county fire department next week, and they always play dirty. Last season, their point guard threw an elbow that nearly broke my nose, and the ref didn't even call it. It's like they get a free pass just because they drive the big red trucks. But yeah, no, I just play my normal games. I'm not running marathons or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating certain foods or restrict your eating beyond what you've already described because you're afraid of gaining weight or because of how it might affect your shape?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I wouldn't say I avoid foods just to avoid them, but... yeah, if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling even a little tighter around the waist, I do get kind of... I don't know, I just start thinking I'm getting soft. Like, if I'm carrying extra weight, I'm going to be slower moving around the shop, sliding under cars, or even just getting up and down the court during league games. \n\nActually, speaking of jeans, I had to take back this pair of Levi's I bought last weekend. They changed the sizing or something, because I always buy the exact same style—the 501s, you know, the classic ones—and they just fit completely wrong now. It's like they're trying to save money on denim by cutting them tighter in the legs. I was talking to the guy at the hardware store about it, and he said the same thing happened with his work boots. He bought the same brand for fifteen years, and suddenly the soles started wearing out in six months because they moved production overseas. It's just like what's happening with car parts, honestly. You buy a replacement water pump now, and half the time the casting is so cheap it cracks when you torque the bolts down. You really have to hunt down the old stock if you want something that's going to last. But anyway, yeah, I just don't want to get soft and slow down, so I keep an eye on things.\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep an eye on things to avoid getting soft, how much of your focus is on your weight or shape compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Compared to how I used to be? I don't really know... I haven't really measured it or anything like that. I guess I just pay attention to it the same as I always do. I haven't noticed a big difference in how much I think about it. \n\nBut man, speaking of how things used to be, working on cars used to be so much simpler. Back when I started out, you could actually open a hood and see the engine, you know? Now? It's all just giant plastic engine covers and a million computer sensors. We had this 2019 Audi in the shop yesterday, and just to get to the oil filter, you have to take off this massive undercarriage shield. It's held on by about fifteen different Torx screws, and half of them were completely stripped out because the last guy who did the lube job used an impact driver on them. I mean, who uses an impact gun on a plastic skid plate? It's just pure laziness. \n\nThe younger guys coming into the shop now, they don't want to take the time to do things by hand or actually feel the threads catch. They just want to plug in the scanner and let the iPad tell them what part to swap out. There's no real diagnosing anymore, it's just throwing parts at a code. It drives me crazy. But anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, I don't know, I just keep an eye on it. I wouldn't say it's some big new focus or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any medical problems or been diagnosed with a condition that could explain your weight loss, such as thyroid issues, diabetes, or gastrointestinal problems?\n\nPatient: Medical problems? No, nothing like that. I've never had any thyroid stuff or diabetes or stomach issues. I'm pretty healthy, all things considered. I mean, my back gives me some trouble now and then, but that's just from leaning over engine bays for twenty years. \n\nActually, speaking of medical stuff, did you hear about that new clinic they opened up over on Elm Street? The one in the old strip mall where the Blockbuster used to be? I drove past it the other day, and they've got this huge glowing sign now. It's so weird seeing it like that, because I remember renting VHS tapes from that exact spot when I was a kid. You'd go in on a Friday night, and if you didn't get there by six, all the new releases were gone. Just empty plastic cases sitting on the shelf mocking you. My buddy Dave used to work there, and he'd always stash a copy of whatever the big action movie was behind the counter for us. Dave actually moved out to Arizona a few years back. Bought a house with a pool and everything. He says the dry heat is great, but I don't know how anyone deals with the sun baking the clear coat right off your car out there. You see these cars from out West, and the paint is just peeling off in sheets on the hood and roof. It's a real shame, especially on the classics. But yeah, no, I don't have any medical conditions or anything causing the weight loss. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself sick or done anything else to get rid of food after eating because you were worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Make myself sick? Like, throw up? No, no, I've never done anything like that. That just seems... I don't know, it's not something I'd do. I mean, the last time I threw up was probably five years ago when I got food poisoning from this taco truck that parked outside the shop. \n\nMan, that was brutal. It was this new truck, bright yellow, and they had these al pastor tacos that smelled amazing. The guys and I decided to try it out for lunch. Big mistake. Half the shop was out the next day. It's funny, though, because the food truck business has just exploded around here lately. Used to be you'd only see them at construction sites, you know, the old \"roach coaches.\" Now they're everywhere, charging like fifteen bucks for a gourmet grilled cheese. My wife loves going to those food truck festivals they have downtown on the weekends. They block off the whole street, and it's just packed with people. Finding parking is a nightmare, though. Last time we went, I had to park like six blocks away in this sketchy alley, and I spent the whole time worrying someone was going to sideswipe my truck. It's a Chevy Silverado, extended cab, so it sticks out a bit. People just don't know how to navigate tight spaces anymore. They rely entirely on those backup cameras, and half the time they're not even looking in their mirrors. I see it all the time at the shop—bumpers all scuffed up because someone backed into a pole while staring at their dashboard screen. But anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, making myself sick. No, never done that.\n\nDoctor: Even though you say you haven't felt overweight, do you ever feel like your body looks different or that certain parts of you are bigger than they should be?\n\nPatient: Bigger than they should be? No, not really. I mean, I look in the mirror and I just look like me, you know? I haven't noticed any parts looking weird or out of proportion. I just try to keep everything tight so I don't get soft. \n\nBut you know what is getting bigger than it should be? The size of these new pickup trucks. It's ridiculous. Have you seen the new heavy-duty models they're putting out? The grilles on those things are practically at eye level for a normal person. We had a guy bring in a 2023 Ram 2500 last week for a brake job, and I swear, I almost needed a stepladder just to check under the hood. And the blind spots on those things! It's no wonder people are always backing into things. The beds are so high up now, you can't even reach over the side to grab a toolbox without pulling a muscle. \n\nBack in the day, a work truck was just that—a work truck. You had your standard cab, an eight-foot bed, and it sat low enough that you could actually load sheets of plywood into it without needing a forklift. Now they're all lifted from the factory, with these massive crew cabs that are nicer inside than my living room. Heated leather seats, giant touchscreens, panoramic sunroofs... it's like driving a luxury yacht down the highway. And the price tags! Guys are taking out eighty-month loans just to afford a truck they're going to use to commute to an office job. It makes no sense. If you're going to haul air, buy a sedan, right? But what was the question? Oh, my body looking different. No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Just staying regular.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in your appetite, energy level, or digestion recently, like feeling full quickly, nausea, or changes in your bowel habits?\n\nPatient: Changes in appetite or energy? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My digestion is fine, no nausea or anything. I just eat what I need to keep going, you know? \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, you wouldn't believe the energy it takes to deal with some of the parts suppliers these days. It used to be you'd call up the local auto parts store, talk to a guy named Bob who'd been there for thirty years, and he'd have the exact alternator you needed sitting on the counter by the time you drove over. Now? Everything's centralized in these massive distribution warehouses. You order a part online, and it's a total crapshoot whether the right thing actually shows up. \n\nJust last Tuesday, I ordered a set of rotors for a Honda Civic. Simple, right? Most common car on the road. The delivery guy shows up, drops off the boxes, and when I open them up, it's a pair of brake drums for a 1998 Ford Ranger. I mean, how do you even mix that up? They don't even look the same! So then you have to get on the phone, wait on hold for twenty minutes listening to that terrible elevator music, just to talk to some customer service rep who doesn't know a spark plug from a lug nut. It just drains the life out of you, dealing with the incompetence. You try to run an efficient shop, keep the bays turning over, but you're constantly bottlenecked by these massive corporate supply chains that can't get a simple order right. It's exhausting. But yeah, physically, my energy is fine. No issues with my stomach or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever weighed yourself recently, and if so, do you know if your weight is below what would be considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, that's kind of why I'm here. I had to do this work physical recently, and the doc there told me my weight was too low for my height. But I don't really see it as a bad thing. I feel a lot lighter on my feet, you know? Makes a huge difference when I'm playing pickup basketball on the weekends, and I definitely don't feel as weighed down when I'm moving around the garage all day. \n\nSpeaking of the garage, man, the floors in that place are brutal on your knees after a while. Concrete just doesn't give. A buddy of mine, Dave, he's been turning wrenches for thirty years, and his knees are completely shot. He just had to get one replaced last month. The recovery on that is no joke. They had him up and walking on it the same day, which sounds crazy to me, but I guess that's how they do it now. He was telling me about the physical therapy, how they basically force you to bend it even when it feels like it's going to snap. Sounds like torture. He's got this machine he has to use at home that just slowly bends and straightens his leg for hours. His wife is going nuts because it makes this constant whining noise. She's been spending half her time out in her garden just to get away from the sound. She grows these massive heirloom tomatoes, though. Brought a basket into the shop before Dave's surgery, and they were incredible. Nothing like a real, homegrown tomato. The ones at the grocery store just taste like water. But anyway, yeah, the physical doc said I was under whatever the normal weight is supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever follow any specific dietary rules or routines to make sure you don’t gain weight, like weighing or measuring your food, avoiding certain ingredients, or tracking calories?\n\nPatient: Rules? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them rules, but I definitely have a routine. Most days I just stick to coffee in the morning. Keeps me moving. Then later on, I might grab a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever the guys ordered for the shop. It's just easier that way. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple routine, you know? \n\nSpeaking of the guys ordering food, we've got this new kid, Tyler, started as a lube tech a couple months ago. Good kid, works hard, but his diet is unbelievable. I swear, I watch him eat a double bacon cheeseburger, large fries, and a liter of soda for lunch every single day. And he's skinny as a rail! It's crazy what your metabolism can do when you're nineteen. But the worst part is the smell. He always leaves his empty fast-food bags sitting on top of the tire balancer, and by two o'clock the whole back corner of the shop smells like stale grease and onions. I've told him a hundred times to throw his trash in the dumpster out back, but it's like talking to a brick wall. Kids today, they just don't have that awareness of their surroundings. \n\nLike the other day, I caught him using a half-inch impact wrench on a plastic oil filter housing. I yelled across the bay, dropped what I was doing, and practically tackled him before he cracked the thing. You crack one of those on a European car, you're waiting three days for the dealership to get the part in, and the customer is screaming at you the whole time. You gotta have a feel for the tools, you know? Hand-tighten the plastic stuff, always. But yeah, as far as my food goes, I just keep it simple. Coffee, maybe half a sandwich. It just works for me.\n\nDoctor: Even though you feel good and are keeping up with work and basketball, is your weight loss something you're actively trying to achieve, or has it happened more than you intended?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I guess I'm trying to keep it down, yeah. It's not like I'm trying to vanish or anything, but if I step on the scale and see it bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tight around the waist, I start getting this thought in my head that I'm getting soft. Like, I'll be slower getting up and down the court, or I won't be able to maneuver around the cars as easy. You really gotta be able to contort yourself when you're working under a dash, you know? \n\nMan, working under the dash is the absolute worst. They cram so much wiring and so many modules up there now, you practically need to be a gymnast just to change a heater core. I had this Chevy Silverado in last week, right? Customer says the blend door actuator is clicking. Classic issue. But to get to the thing, you have to pull the entire dash assembly apart. And I'm laying on my back across the driver's seat, my head down by the pedals, trying to reach up blind with a quarter-inch ratchet to get this one tiny screw out. And of course, the screw strips. They use these cheap, soft metal screws that strip if you even look at them wrong. So now I'm stuck, upside down, blood rushing to my head, trying to figure out how to drill out a screw in a space where I can't even fit my hand. \n\nTook me three hours just to get that one part out. My neck was stiff for two days after that. I ended up having to use my wife's heating pad just to be able to turn my head enough to check my blind spot driving to work. She wasn't thrilled about that, she uses it for her lower back. She's been doing this new yoga class at the community center, and I think she pulled something trying to do one of those crazy poses. She says it's supposed to be relaxing, but she always comes home complaining about how much she hurts. I don't get it. But yeah, if I put on weight, I'd just feel too sluggish to deal with all that awkward positioning at the shop.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight loss was completely intentional, or did you lose more weight than you originally aimed for?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I had a specific number in mind, honestly. I just knew I wanted to lean out a bit, and I kept going until I felt right. I guess I haven't really thought about whether I lost more than I planned. I just kind of keep an eye on how my clothes fit and how I feel on the court. \n\nSpeaking of the court, we had this insane game last Sunday. We play at this outdoor court down by the middle school, and the asphalt is getting pretty chewed up in places. Anyway, we were playing this team of guys who usually play over at the Y, and they think they're hot stuff, right? They show up in matching jerseys and everything. It's pickup, for crying out loud. But they had this one guy, tall, maybe six-four, who kept trying to post me up. I might not be the biggest guy out there, especially now, but I know how to hold my ground. \n\nHe goes up for a rebound, right? And he comes down awkward on one of those patched spots on the asphalt. You could hear the pop from half-court. Rolled his ankle so bad he couldn't even put weight on it. We had to help him hobble over to his car. It's crazy how fast an injury like that can happen. Makes you appreciate being light on your feet, you know? Less weight coming down on those joints. That's what I keep telling my buddy Dave with his new knee. He needs to drop a few pounds if he wants that hardware to last. He's a big guy, always has been. Loves his barbecue. He got this new smoker for Father's Day, one of those fancy pellet ones that you can control with an app on your phone. He's been smoking briskets every weekend. Brings the leftovers into the shop on Mondays. Smells incredible, but man, that stuff is just pure fat and salt. I tell him he's gonna clog his arteries before that new knee even gets broken in, but he just laughs it off. But yeah, I don't know if I lost more than I meant to, I just know I like where I'm at.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever count or track the calories in your food, or follow any strict dietary rules to make sure you don’t eat too much?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really count calories or anything like that. I just have my rules, I guess. Like I said before, most days I keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever the guys ordered. It's just easier. With the shop always running late and then going home to a busy house, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules, you know? It keeps things manageable. \n\nSpeaking of the house being busy, my youngest, Leo, he just started playing the drums. And I mean, I love the kid, but he has zero rhythm right now. Just zero. He just bangs on the snare like he's trying to punish it. We had to move the drum kit out to the garage because the dog was having panic attacks every time he sat down to play. So now, I get home from a ten-hour shift listening to air compressors and impact guns all day, and I pull into the driveway, and it sounds like a construction site in my own garage. I tried to show him some basic beats, you know, just a simple four-four time, but he just wants to hit the cymbals as hard as he can. I'm thinking about getting him some of those practice pads that muffle the sound, or maybe just soundproofing the garage doors. I saw this guy on YouTube who used old moving blankets and some acoustic foam to soundproof his garage for a band practice space. Seemed pretty straightforward. You just need a heavy-duty staple gun and some patience. I might have to run to the hardware store this weekend and see what they have. But yeah, sticking to my food rules just helps me deal with all the chaos, I guess."
